### Keep Focus

Only display information relevant to the current task.

---

### Be Easy to Exit

Users should never feel trapped.

Closing an overlay should always be simple and predictable.

---

# 10.3 Overlay Hierarchy

React Commerce uses four primary overlay types.

```text
Tooltip

↓

Popover

↓

Drawer

↓

Modal
```

Choose the least disruptive overlay capable of completing the task.

---

# 10.4 When to Use Each Overlay

| Overlay | Best For |
|----------|----------|
| Tooltip | Brief explanation |
| Popover | Small interactive content |
| Drawer | Medium or complex workflows |
| Modal | Critical confirmation or focused tasks |

Never use a Modal when a Tooltip would suffice.

---

# 10.5 Tooltip

Purpose:

Provide additional information without interrupting the user.

Examples:

- Icon explanations
- Keyboard shortcuts
- Validation hints
- Status descriptions

---

Tooltip Example

```text
      ⓘ

 ┌──────────────────────────┐
 │ AI Confidence Score      │
 │ Indicates prediction     │
 │ accuracy.                │
 └──────────────────────────┘
```

Tooltips are informational only.

Never place buttons or forms inside tooltips.

---

# 10.6 Tooltip Specifications

| Property | Value |
|----------|-------|
| Max Width | 280px |
| Padding | 8px 12px |
| Radius | Radius SM |
| Trigger | Hover / Focus |
| Delay | 300ms |

Tooltips disappear automatically when focus leaves the element.

---

# 10.7 Popover

Purpose

Display lightweight interactive content.

Examples

- Quick Filters
- Date Picker
- Emoji Picker
- Color Picker
- User Menu

Popover Example

```text
┌─────────────────────────────┐

Filter By

☐ WhatsApp

☑ Email

☐ SMS

Apply

└─────────────────────────────┘
```

Popovers should remain lightweight.

---

# 10.8 Popover Specifications

| Property | Value |
|----------|-------|
| Max Width | 360px |
| Auto Position | Yes |
| Shadow | Medium |
| Close on Outside Click | Yes |
| Keyboard Accessible | Yes |

Avoid placing long forms inside popovers.

---

# 10.9 Drawer

Drawers are the preferred overlay for enterprise workflows.

React Commerce should use Drawers whenever users need to:

- Create resources
- Edit records
- View details
- Configure settings
- Review information

without leaving the current page.

---

# 10.10 Drawer Example

```text
┌────────────────────────────────────────────┐

Contacts List

────────────────────────────────────────────

                          ┌─────────────────┐

                          │ Create Contact  │

                          │                 │

                          │ Name            │

                          │ Phone           │

                          │ Company         │

                          │                 │

                          │ Cancel   Save   │

                          └─────────────────┘
```

The underlying page remains visible.

This preserves context.

---

# 10.11 Drawer Sizes

| Size | Width |
|------|--------|
| Small | 400px |
| Medium | 560px |
| Large | 720px |
| Extra Large | 960px |
| Full Screen | 100% |

Recommended default:

```text
560px
```

---

# 10.12 Drawer Anatomy

```text
Header

──────────────────────────

Content

──────────────────────────

Footer Actions
```

Header includes:

- Title
- Description (optional)
- Close Button

Footer includes:

- Secondary Action
- Primary Action

---

# 10.13 Drawer Behavior

Opening

```text
Slide from Right

180ms
```

Closing

```text
Slide Out

150ms
```

Rules

- Preserve scroll position of the underlying page.
- Prevent accidental dismissal during unsaved edits.
- Support keyboard navigation.

---

# 10.14 Modal

Purpose

Interrupt the user only when absolutely necessary.

Examples

- Delete Workspace
- Confirm Payment
- Permission Changes
- Security Verification

Modals demand complete attention.

---

# 10.15 Modal Example

```text
────────────────────────────

Delete Campaign

This action cannot be undone.

Cancel

Delete

────────────────────────────
```

A modal should answer:

- What happened?
- What happens next?
- How do I continue?

---

# 10.16 Modal Sizes

| Size | Width |
|------|--------|
| Small | 400px |
| Medium | 600px |
| Large | 800px |

Avoid fullscreen modals unless absolutely necessary.

---

# 10.17 Confirmation Dialogs

Confirmation dialogs should be reserved for irreversible actions.

Examples

✔ Delete

✔ Remove Workspace

✔ Reset AI Model

✔ Revoke API Key

Do **not** ask users to confirm:

- Saving
- Editing
- Viewing
- Searching
- Filtering

Too many confirmations reduce productivity.

---

# 10.18 Overlay Stacking

Only one major overlay should be active.

Recommended

```text
Page

↓

Drawer

↓

Popover

↓

Tooltip
```

Avoid

```text
Modal

↓

Drawer

↓

Popover

↓

Modal
```

Users should never manage multiple competing overlays.

---

# 10.19 Closing Rules

Every overlay must support:

✔ Close Button

✔ Escape Key

✔ Outside Click (except destructive workflows)

✔ Cancel Action

Never force users to refresh the page to exit an overlay.

---

# 10.20 Unsaved Changes

If the user has unsaved work:

Example

```text
Discard Changes?

Your edits haven't been saved.

Continue Editing

Discard
```

Never silently discard user input.

---

# 10.21 Overlay Background

When an overlay opens:

- Background becomes inactive.
- Reduce opacity slightly.
- Prevent unintended interaction.

Recommended backdrop opacity:

```text
40%
```

The backdrop should focus attention without completely hiding context.

---

# 10.22 Focus Management

Opening an overlay should:

1. Move keyboard focus into the overlay.
2. Trap focus within the overlay.
3. Restore focus to the triggering element when closed.

Users should never lose their place.

---

# 10.23 Mobile Behavior

Desktop

```text
Drawer
```

↓

Mobile

```text
Full Screen Sheet
```

Small screens benefit from maximizing available space.

Avoid narrow floating panels on mobile.

---

# 10.24 Accessibility

Every overlay must support:

✔ ARIA Roles

✔ Focus Trap

✔ Escape Key

✔ Screen Readers

✔ Visible Focus

✔ Keyboard Navigation

✔ Announced Title

Accessibility is essential because overlays temporarily interrupt normal page flow.

---

# 10.25 Motion

Recommended animations

Tooltip

```text
Fade

120ms
```

Popover

```text
Fade + Scale

150ms
```

Drawer

```text
Slide

180ms
```

Modal

```text
Fade + Scale

200ms
```

Motion should reinforce hierarchy.

Never feel theatrical.

---

# 10.26 React Component API

Tooltip

```tsx
<Tooltip

content="AI Confidence Score"

>

<InfoIcon />

</Tooltip>
```

Drawer

```tsx
<Drawer

title="Create Contact"

size="md"

open={open}

onClose={handleClose}

>

<ContactForm />

</Drawer>
```

Modal

```tsx
<Modal

title="Delete Campaign"

variant="danger"

>

<Confirmation />

</Modal>
```

---

# 10.27 CSS Variables

```css
--drawer-width-md:560px;

--modal-width-md:600px;

--popover-radius:12px;

--tooltip-radius:8px;

--overlay-backdrop:rgba(0,0,0,.40);
```

---

# 10.28 Do's & Don'ts

## Do

✔ Use drawers for create/edit workflows.

✔ Keep tooltips short.

✔ Use popovers for lightweight interactions.

✔ Reserve modals for critical actions.

✔ Preserve user context.

✔ Support keyboard navigation.

✔ Restore focus when overlays close.

---

## Don't

✘ Place long forms inside popovers.

✘ Stack multiple modals.

✘ Interrupt users unnecessarily.

✘ Hide close controls.

✘ Discard unsaved work silently.

✘ Use modals for simple confirmations like "Saved Successfully."

---

# 10.29 Chapter Summary

Overlays allow React Commerce users to perform complex tasks without leaving their current workflow.

By selecting the appropriate overlay—Tooltip, Popover, Drawer, or Modal—the platform balances productivity with clarity, ensuring users remain focused while minimizing unnecessary navigation.

Every overlay should preserve context, support accessibility, and provide clear entry and exit points.

---

# React Commerce Design Standard

> **Choose the least disruptive overlay that enables task completion. Preserve context, protect user work, and ensure every overlay is accessible, predictable, and easy to dismiss. Drawers are the default choice for enterprise workflows; modals are reserved for high-impact decisions.**

---

# End of Chapter 10 — Overlays (Modals, Drawers, Popovers & Tooltips)

---

## End of Volume II — Core Components

---

# React Commerce Design System & Frontend Engineering Handbook

# Volume III — Advanced Components

# Chapter 11 — AI Components & Conversational Interfaces

---

# 11.1 Introduction

Artificial Intelligence is no longer a feature.

It is becoming the primary interface between users and enterprise software.

Unlike traditional UI, AI interfaces are conversational, adaptive, and context-aware.

Instead of navigating menus to perform dozens of actions, users can simply ask:

> "Create a WhatsApp campaign for premium customers."

or

> "Summarize this conversation."

or

> "Show contacts that haven't replied in 30 days."

The responsibility of the interface is no longer just displaying data—it is understanding intent.

React Commerce treats AI as a first-class design system component rather than an add-on.

---

# 11.2 AI Design Principles

Every AI experience follows six principles.

## Transparency

Users should always know when they are interacting with AI.

Never disguise AI as a human operator.

---

## Explainability

Whenever AI makes a recommendation, users should understand why.

Example

```text
Suggested Segment

High-Value Customers

Reason

Purchased more than 5 times in the last 90 days.
```

---

## Human Control

AI assists.

Humans decide.

Critical actions always require user approval.

---

## Context Awareness

AI should understand:

- Current page
- Selected contact
- Current campaign
- Recent actions
- Workspace settings

Without asking repeatedly.

---

## Progressive Assistance

Offer help only when it adds value.

Avoid interrupting workflows.

---

## Trust

Confidence grows when AI is predictable, explainable, and reversible.

---

# 11.3 AI Interface Hierarchy

```text
AI Assistant

↓

Conversation Panel

↓

Prompt Composer

↓

AI Response

↓

Suggested Actions

↓

Execution
```

Each layer should clearly communicate its purpose.

---

# 11.4 AI Entry Points

AI should be accessible throughout React Commerce.

Common entry points include:

- Global AI Button
- Command Palette
- Inbox Assistant
- Flow Builder
- Analytics
- Content Planner
- Contact 360
- Broadcast Builder

Users should never wonder where AI is available.

---

# 11.5 AI Assistant Panel

The assistant panel is the primary conversational workspace.

Recommended layout

```text
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐

🤖 React AI

──────────────────────────────────────────────

Conversation

Conversation

Conversation

──────────────────────────────────────────────

Prompt Input

──────────────────────────────────────────────

Suggestions

└──────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

The panel should remain available without disrupting the current screen.

---

# 11.6 Component Blueprint — AI Assistant

## Anatomy

```text
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ 🤖 React AI                    ×             │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Suggested Prompts                          │
│────────────────────────────────────────────│
│ "Summarize today's conversations"          │
│                                            │
│ User Message                              │
│                                            │
│ AI Response                               │
│                                            │
│ Suggested Actions                          │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ✨ Ask React AI...                 ➤        │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

## Layout Specifications

| Element | Size |
|----------|------|
| Width | 420px |
| Header | 64px |
| Input Area | 64px Minimum |
| Message Gap | 16px |
| Bubble Radius | 16px |
| Suggested Prompt Height | 44px |

---

# 11.7 Prompt Composer

The prompt composer should feel familiar to modern messaging applications.

Support:

✔ Multi-line input

✔ Keyboard shortcuts

✔ File attachment

✔ Image upload

✔ Voice input (future)

✔ Slash commands

Example

```text
Ask React AI...

________________________

Send (Ctrl + Enter)
```

---

# 11.8 Suggested Prompts

Empty conversations should guide users.

Example

```text
✨ Summarize today's inbox

✨ Create a campaign

✨ Find inactive customers

✨ Explain analytics

✨ Generate WhatsApp template
```

Suggested prompts reduce blank-page anxiety.

---

# 11.9 AI Response Card

Every AI response follows a consistent structure.

```text
AI Response

──────────────────────────────

Generated Content

──────────────────────────────

Actions

Copy

Insert

Edit

Regenerate
```

Users should never be forced to copy text manually if an action exists.

---

# 11.10 AI Message Types

Supported response formats include:

| Type | Example |
|--------|----------|
| Text | Summary |
| Table | Analytics |
| Checklist | Tasks |
| Code | API Snippets |
| Flow | Automation |
| Chart | Trends |
| Card | Recommendation |

The interface should adapt to response type automatically.

---

# 11.11 Confidence Indicators

When appropriate, display confidence.

Example

```text
Confidence

92%
```

Low confidence responses should encourage verification.

Example

```text
This recommendation may require manual review.
```

---

# 11.12 Suggested Actions

Every AI response should lead naturally to action.

Example

```text
Generate Broadcast

↓

Review Audience

↓

Schedule Campaign
```

Suggested actions reduce friction between insight and execution.

---

# 11.13 AI Workflow Pattern

```text
User Prompt

↓

Thinking

↓

Response

↓

Review

↓

Approve

↓

Execute

↓

Confirmation
```

AI should never skip the review stage for important actions.

---

# 11.14 Streaming Responses

Large responses should stream progressively.

Benefits

- Faster perceived performance
- Greater transparency
- Reduced waiting time

Avoid displaying large responses all at once after long delays.

---

# 11.15 Regeneration

Users should easily regenerate responses.

Example

```text
Regenerate

↓

Alternative Response
```

Maintain conversation history for comparison.

---

# 11.16 Editable Responses

Generated content should remain editable.

Example

```text
Generated Broadcast

↓

Edit

↓

Publish
```

AI output is a draft—not a final decision.

---

# 11.17 AI Loading State

Instead of generic spinners:

Display progress.

Example

```text
React AI is analyzing your conversations...

████████░░░░
```

If processing exceeds five seconds, provide contextual messaging.

---

# 11.18 AI Error State

Errors should explain the problem.

Example

```text
Unable to generate a response.

The AI service is temporarily unavailable.

Retry
```

Never expose raw API errors to end users.

---

# 11.19 Conversation Memory

The assistant should remember relevant context during a session.

Example

```text
User

Create another campaign like yesterday.

AI

Using yesterday's campaign settings...
```

Visible context improves predictability and user trust.

---

# 11.20 AI Safety

Before executing critical operations:

Require confirmation.

Examples

- Delete Contacts
- Send Campaign
- Archive Inbox
- Modify Billing
- Remove Team Members

AI suggestions are not AI permissions.

---

# 11.21 Keyboard Shortcuts

| Shortcut | Action |
|-----------|--------|
| Ctrl + K | Open AI |
| Ctrl + Enter | Send Prompt |
| Esc | Close Panel |
| ↑ | Previous Prompt |
| ↓ | Next Suggestion |

Power users should complete AI workflows without a mouse.

---

# 11.22 Accessibility

AI interfaces support:

✔ Screen Readers

✔ Keyboard Navigation

✔ Focus Management

✔ Live Region Announcements

✔ High Contrast

✔ Adjustable Text Size

AI conversations should be as accessible as traditional interfaces.

---

# 11.23 Motion

Recommended animations

Message

```text
Fade + Slide

160ms
```

Typing Indicator

```text
Pulse

Loop
```

Panel

```text
Slide In

180ms
```

Motion should communicate activity—not entertainment.

---

# 11.24 React Component API

```tsx
<AIAssistant

workspace={workspace}

conversation={messages}

onSubmit={handlePrompt}

suggestions={suggestedPrompts}

streaming

/>
```

Message

```tsx
<AIMessage

role="assistant"

actions={["Copy","Insert","Regenerate"]}

/>
```

---

# 11.25 CSS Variables

```css
--ai-panel-width:420px;

--ai-message-gap:16px;

--ai-radius:16px;

--ai-input-height:64px;

--ai-stream-speed:160ms;
```

---

# 11.26 Do's & Don'ts

## Do

✔ Clearly identify AI-generated content.

✔ Explain recommendations.

✔ Stream long responses.

✔ Allow editing before execution.

✔ Preserve conversation history.

✔ Provide suggested actions.

✔ Confirm high-risk operations.

---

## Don't

✘ Present AI as infallible.

✘ Execute destructive actions automatically.

✘ Hide confidence or uncertainty.

✘ Interrupt users unnecessarily.

✘ Lock generated content from editing.

✘ Expose technical model errors.

---

# 11.27 Chapter Summary

AI Components transform React Commerce from a traditional enterprise platform into an intelligent workspace.

By standardizing conversational interfaces, response cards, prompt composers, suggested actions, and AI-assisted workflows, the platform enables users to accomplish complex tasks through natural language while maintaining transparency, control, and trust.

A well-designed AI interface does not replace users—it amplifies their productivity.

---

# React Commerce Design Standard

> **AI should feel like an intelligent collaborator, not an autonomous decision-maker. Every AI interaction must be transparent, explainable, editable, and user-controlled, enabling faster work without sacrificing trust or accountability.**

---

# End of Chapter 11 — AI Components & Conversational Interfaces

---

# React Commerce Design System & Frontend Engineering Handbook

# Volume III — Advanced Components

# Chapter 12 — Advanced Data Components (Tables, Trees, Kanban & Timelines)

---

# 12.1 Introduction

Enterprise applications are built around data.

As organizations grow, data becomes increasingly interconnected, hierarchical, and dynamic.

Simple tables are no longer sufficient.

Users need to:

- Explore relationships
- Manage workflows
- Track progress
- Compare information
- Analyze history
- Organize complex structures

React Commerce provides advanced data components that allow users to work efficiently with large-scale business information while maintaining clarity and performance.

---

# 12.2 Design Philosophy

Advanced data components follow six principles.

## Discoverability

Users should quickly locate the information they need.

---

## Scalability

Components should perform equally well with:

- 10 records
- 1,000 records
- 1 million records

---

## Progressive Disclosure

Reveal complexity gradually.

Never overwhelm users.

---

## Action-Oriented

Viewing data should naturally lead to meaningful actions.

---

## Performance

Large datasets should remain responsive.

---

## Consistency

All advanced components should behave predictably.

---

# 12.3 Component Hierarchy

```text
Data Table

↓

Grouped Table

↓

Tree View

↓

Kanban

↓

Timeline

↓

Data Explorer
```

Each component solves a different organizational problem.

---

# 12.4 Enterprise Data Table

The Enterprise Table is the most frequently used component in React Commerce.

Used in:

- Contacts
- Campaigns
- Templates
- Orders
- Analytics
- AI Agents
- Broadcasts

---

## Blueprint

```text
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

Toolbar

Search

Filters

Export

Columns

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

☐ Name            Status        Owner         Updated         Actions

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

☐ Rahul Sharma    Active        Admin         2 mins ago      ⋮

☐ Priya Singh     Pending       Sales         Yesterday       ⋮

☐ Amit Kumar      Archived      Marketing     Jul 24          ⋮

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Pagination

└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

## Table Anatomy

```text
Toolbar

↓

Column Header

↓

Data Rows

↓

Bulk Actions

↓

Pagination
```

---

# 12.5 Table Toolbar

The toolbar provides high-frequency actions.

Recommended actions

- Search
- Filters
- Sort
- Export
- Import
- Column Settings
- Density
- Refresh

The toolbar should remain sticky during scrolling.

---

# 12.6 Column Management

Users should customize visible columns.

Capabilities

✔ Hide

✔ Show

✔ Resize

✔ Reorder

✔ Pin Left

✔ Pin Right

✔ Save Preferences

Example

```text
Columns

☑ Name

☑ Status

☑ Owner

☑ Created

☐ Notes
```

User preferences should persist across sessions.

---

# 12.7 Row Selection

Tables support three selection modes.

Single

```text
☐
```

Multiple

```text
☑ ☑ ☑
```

Select All

```text
☑ All 2,491 Contacts
```

Bulk selection should remain obvious.

---

# 12.8 Bulk Action Bar

When rows are selected:

```text
────────────────────────────

42 Contacts Selected

Assign

Tag

Export

Delete

────────────────────────────
```

Hide the toolbar when nothing is selected.

---

# 12.9 Sorting

Supported sorting

- Ascending
- Descending
- Multi-column

Example

```text
Status ↑

Created ↓
```

Sorting indicators should remain visible.

---

# 12.10 Filtering

Filtering should support:

- Quick Filters
- Advanced Filters
- Saved Filters
- AI Filters (Future)

Example

```text
Status

Active

AND

Country

India

AND

Last Purchase

30 Days
```

Always display active filters.

---

# 12.11 Grouped Tables

Users often think in categories.

Grouping organizes rows.

Example

```text
Marketing

▼

John

Rahul

Priya

────────────

Sales

▼

Ankit

Deepak
```

Collapsed groups improve navigation.

---

# 12.12 Tree View

Tree views display hierarchical information.

Examples

- Folder Structure
- Automation Flows
- Knowledge Base
- Categories

Blueprint

```text
▼ Workspace

    ▼ Marketing

        Campaign A

        Campaign B

    ▼ Sales

        Campaign C
```

---

# 12.13 Tree Interaction

Supported interactions

✔ Expand

✔ Collapse

✔ Drag

✔ Rename

✔ Context Menu

✔ Keyboard Navigation

Avoid expanding every node by default.

---

# 12.14 Kanban Board

Kanban visualizes workflow status.

Ideal for

- Leads
- Support Tickets
- Tasks
- Content Approval

---

## Blueprint

```text
┌────────────┬────────────┬────────────┬────────────┐

New

Qualified

Negotiation

Won

────────────

Lead

Lead

Lead

────────────

Lead

Lead

────────────

└────────────┴────────────┴────────────┴────────────┘
```

Each card represents one business object.

---

# 12.15 Kanban Card

```text
Rahul Sharma

Enterprise

₹18K Opportunity

Tomorrow

Assigned: Priya
```

Each card includes:

- Title
- Status
- Metadata
- Priority
- Assignee

---

# 12.16 Drag & Drop

Kanban supports drag-and-drop.

Interaction

```text
Card

↓

Dragging

↓

Target Column

↓

Drop

↓

Confirmation
```

Movement should animate smoothly.

---

# 12.17 Timeline

Timelines display events chronologically.

Example

```text
Created Contact

↓

Campaign Sent

↓

Conversation Started

↓

Order Placed

↓

Repeat Purchase
```

Users should understand history at a glance.

---

# 12.18 Timeline Card

```text
● Broadcast Sent

2:45 PM

Campaign

Summer Sale

By

Rahul
```

Every event includes:

- Timestamp
- Actor
- Event
- Category

---

# 12.19 Data Explorer

The Data Explorer allows users to inspect records without leaving the current page.

Blueprint

```text
Table

↓

Select Row

↓

Side Panel Opens

↓

Details

↓

Actions
```

This reduces navigation and preserves context.

---

# 12.20 Expandable Rows

Rows may contain secondary information.

Example

```text
▶ Rahul Sharma
```

↓

```text
Orders

Conversations

Campaigns

AI Summary
```

Expandable rows reduce unnecessary page transitions.

---

# 12.21 Nested Tables

Nested tables display related collections.

Example

```text
Contact

↓

Orders

↓

Products
```

Limit nesting to two levels.

Deep nesting reduces readability.

---

# 12.22 Performance

Large datasets require optimization.

Recommendations

✔ Virtual Scrolling

✔ Lazy Loading

✔ Incremental Rendering

✔ Server-side Pagination

✔ Background Fetching

Performance should remain consistent regardless of dataset size.

---

# 12.23 Empty States

Example

```text
No Leads Found

Try adjusting your filters.

Reset Filters
```

Provide recovery actions.

---

# 12.24 Accessibility

Advanced data components support:

✔ Screen Readers

✔ Keyboard Navigation

✔ Focus Management

✔ Row Announcements

✔ Column Headers

✔ Drag Alternatives

Every interaction should remain accessible without a mouse.

---

# 12.25 Motion

Recommended animations

Row Expand

```text
150ms
```

Tree Expand

```text
180ms
```

Card Drag

```text
Real-time
```

Timeline Entry

```text
Fade

160ms
```

Motion should clarify state changes.

---

# 12.26 React Component API

Enterprise Table

```tsx
<DataTable

rows={contacts}

columns={columns}

selection

pagination

sorting

filtering

virtualized

/>
```

Kanban

```tsx
<KanbanBoard

columns={pipeline}

onMove={handleMove}

/>
```

Tree

```tsx
<Tree

nodes={folders}

expandable

draggable

/>
```

---

# 12.27 CSS Variables

```css
--table-row-height:48px;

--table-toolbar-height:64px;

--kanban-column-width:320px;

--timeline-gap:20px;

--tree-indent:24px;
```

---

# 12.28 Do's & Don'ts

## Do

✔ Support bulk operations.

✔ Keep toolbars sticky.

✔ Persist user preferences.

✔ Use virtualization for large datasets.

✔ Display active filters clearly.

✔ Make drag-and-drop optional.

✔ Preserve user context.

---

## Don't

✘ Overload tables with excessive columns.

✘ Expand every tree node by default.

✘ Nest tables excessively.

✘ Require mouse-only interactions.

✘ Lose selections after filtering.

✘ Hide important metadata.

---

# 12.29 Chapter Summary

Advanced Data Components empower users to organize, explore, and act upon complex business information with speed and confidence.

By standardizing enterprise tables, grouped views, tree structures, Kanban boards, timelines, and data explorers, React Commerce transforms large datasets into intuitive, actionable experiences.

These components prioritize scalability, performance, and usability, ensuring that users can work efficiently regardless of the size or complexity of their data.

---

# React Commerce Design Standard

> **Advanced data components should reveal complexity without creating confusion. Every table, tree, Kanban board, or timeline must be fast, scalable, accessible, and action-oriented, enabling users to move seamlessly from information to decision-making.**

---

# End of Chapter 12 — Advanced Data Components (Tables, Trees, Kanban & Timelines)

---

# React Commerce Design System & Frontend Engineering Handbook

# Volume III — Advanced Components

# Chapter 13 — Dashboard & Analytics Components

---

# 13.1 Introduction

Dashboards transform raw business data into actionable intelligence.

For enterprise users, dashboards are often the first screen they see every day.

A well-designed dashboard answers five questions within seconds:

- What is happening?
- Why is it happening?
- What changed?
- What requires attention?
- What should I do next?

React Commerce dashboards are designed to support rapid decision-making through clear information hierarchy, contextual insights, and interactive exploration.

---

# 13.2 Dashboard Design Philosophy

Every dashboard follows six guiding principles.

## Information Before Decoration

Visual clarity is more important than visual complexity.

---

## Progressive Exploration

Show high-level metrics first.

Allow users to drill down into details.

---

## Action-Oriented

Every important metric should lead to a meaningful action.

---

## Real-Time Awareness

Users should understand how current the displayed information is.

---

## Personalization

Users should customize dashboards according to their role.

---

## Performance

Dashboards should feel instantaneous, even with large datasets.

---

# 13.3 Dashboard Hierarchy

Every dashboard follows a consistent layout.

```text
Global Filters

↓

KPI Cards

↓

Charts

↓

Detailed Tables

↓

Recent Activity

↓

Recommended Actions
```

This hierarchy supports quick scanning followed by deeper analysis.

---

# 13.4 Dashboard Blueprint

```text
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

Dashboard Title

Global Date Filter

Export

Refresh

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

KPI Cards

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Charts

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Tables

──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

Recent Activity

Recommended Actions

└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

# 13.5 Dashboard Header

The header provides global context.

Include:

- Dashboard Title
- Date Range
- Workspace Selector
- Export
- Refresh
- Share
- Settings

The header should remain sticky during scrolling.

---

# 13.6 KPI Cards

KPI cards summarize the most important business metrics.

Example

```text
Revenue

$128,420

↑ 14%

Compared to Last Month
```

Each KPI includes:

- Metric
- Label
- Trend
- Comparison
- Time Period

Optional

- Sparkline
- Goal Progress

---

# 13.7 KPI Card Blueprint

```text
┌────────────────────────────┐

Revenue

$128,420

↑ 14%

Last 30 Days

└────────────────────────────┘
```

Specifications

| Property | Value |
|----------|------:|
| Width | Flexible |
| Min Height | 140px |
| Padding | 24px |
| Border Radius | Large |
| Gap | 16px |

---

# 13.8 Dashboard Filters

Filters affect the dashboard globally.

Common filters

- Date Range
- Channel
- Team
- Campaign
- Region
- Product
- AI Agent

Always display active filters.

Example

```text
Date

Last 30 Days

Channel

WhatsApp

Region

India
```

---

# 13.9 Date Range Selector

Every analytics dashboard should support:

- Today
- Yesterday
- Last 7 Days
- Last 30 Days
- Last Quarter
- Custom Range

Display the selected range prominently.

---

# 13.10 Charts

Charts reveal patterns.

Tables reveal details.

Use charts when comparing:

- Trends
- Distribution
- Growth
- Relationships

Never replace tables entirely.

---

# 13.11 Line Charts

Ideal for:

- Revenue Trends
- Conversations
- Campaign Performance
- Message Volume

Example

```text
Revenue

│

│      ╭────╮

│   ╭──╯    ╰──╮

│───╯──────────╰──────
```

Line charts emphasize change over time.

---

# 13.12 Bar Charts

Use for categorical comparison.

Example

```text
WhatsApp

█████████

Email

██████

SMS

███
```

Limit categories to maintain readability.

---

# 13.13 Pie & Donut Charts

Suitable only for simple proportions.

Example

```text
Channel Distribution

WhatsApp

65%

Email

25%

SMS

10%
```

Avoid more than five segments.

---

# 13.14 Area Charts

Use when emphasizing cumulative growth.

Examples

- Active Users
- Revenue
- Orders

Area charts communicate overall volume effectively.

---

# 13.15 Funnel Charts

Funnels visualize conversion.

Example

```text
Visitors

↓

Leads

↓

Qualified

↓

Customers

↓

Repeat Buyers
```

Display both percentages and absolute values.

---

# 13.16 Heatmaps

Heatmaps identify activity concentration.

Examples

- Message Activity
- Agent Availability
- Customer Response Time

Provide legends for interpretation.

---

# 13.17 Geographic Maps

Maps display regional information.

Examples

- Sales by State
- Customer Distribution
- Delivery Coverage

Maps should always include an alternative table view.

---

# 13.18 Data Tables

Every chart should support:

```text
View Data
```

Users often require exact values.

Switching between visualization and table should be seamless.

---

# 13.19 Drill Down

Metrics should be interactive.

Example

```text
Revenue

↓

Click

↓

Orders

↓

Order Details
```

Users should move naturally from summary to detail.

---

# 13.20 Dashboard Widgets

Dashboards consist of modular widgets.

Common widgets

- KPI
- Chart
- Table
- Timeline
- AI Insight
- Leaderboard
- Calendar

Widgets should remain independently reusable.

---

# 13.21 Widget Personalization

Users should customize dashboards.

Supported capabilities

✔ Rearrange

✔ Resize

✔ Hide

✔ Save Layout

✔ Reset Default

Personalization improves productivity.

---

# 13.22 AI Insight Card

AI transforms analytics into recommendations.

Blueprint

```text
🤖 AI Insight

Campaign performance declined by 18%.

Primary reason:

Lower customer engagement during weekends.

Suggested Action

Schedule campaigns between Tuesday and Thursday.
```

Every AI insight should explain:

- Observation
- Cause
- Recommendation

---

# 13.23 Recent Activity

Activity widgets summarize recent business events.

Example

```text
Campaign Published

10 Minutes Ago

────────────────

AI Agent Updated

25 Minutes Ago

────────────────

New Broadcast Scheduled

Yesterday
```

Recent activity provides operational awareness.

---

# 13.24 Empty Analytics

Example

```text
No Data Available

Try expanding the selected date range.

Reset Filters
```

Empty dashboards should guide users toward meaningful data.

---

# 13.25 Loading States

Dashboard loading should use:

- Skeleton Cards
- Skeleton Charts
- Skeleton Tables

Avoid displaying empty containers while loading.

---

# 13.26 Real-Time Updates

Dashboards should communicate freshness.

Example

```text
Updated

2 Minutes Ago
```

Provide manual refresh alongside automatic updates.

---

# 13.27 Accessibility

Analytics components support:

✔ Screen Readers

✔ Keyboard Navigation

✔ High Contrast

✔ Chart Descriptions

✔ Table Alternatives

✔ Focus Visibility

Never rely solely on color to communicate information.

---

# 13.28 Motion

Recommended animations

KPI Count

```text
Count Up

400ms
```

Chart Render

```text
Fade + Draw

300ms
```

Widget Rearrangement

```text
200ms
```

Animations should reinforce comprehension.

---

# 13.29 React Component API

Dashboard

```tsx
<Dashboard>

<KPICard />

<Chart />

<DataTable />

<AIInsight />

</Dashboard>
```

KPI Card

```tsx
<KPICard

title="Revenue"

value="$128,420"

trend="+14%"

/>
```

Chart

```tsx
<LineChart

data={analytics}

/>
```

---

# 13.30 CSS Variables

```css
--dashboard-gap:24px;

--widget-padding:24px;

--kpi-height:140px;

--chart-min-height:320px;

--dashboard-header-height:72px;
```

---

# 13.31 Do's & Don'ts

## Do

✔ Prioritize key metrics.

✔ Keep dashboards customizable.

✔ Support drill-down exploration.

✔ Display update timestamps.

✔ Pair charts with tables.

✔ Provide AI-powered insights.

✔ Keep layouts responsive.

---

## Don't

✘ Display every available metric.

✘ Use decorative charts.

✘ Hide filter context.

✘ Rely only on visualizations.

✘ Overcrowd widgets.

✘ Animate excessively.

✘ Force users into fixed layouts.

---

# 13.32 Chapter Summary

Dashboards are the operational command center of React Commerce.

By combining KPI cards, interactive charts, customizable widgets, AI-generated insights, and detailed tables, dashboards transform business data into actionable intelligence.

A well-designed dashboard enables users to identify opportunities, detect problems, and make informed decisions within seconds.

---

# React Commerce Design Standard

> **Dashboards should present the right information at the right level of detail. Every metric must provide context, every visualization should support decision-making, and every insight should naturally lead to action. Analytics should explain not only what happened, but also why it happened and what users can do next.**

---

# End of Chapter 13 — Dashboard & Analytics Components

# React Commerce Design System & Frontend Engineering Handbook

# Volume III — Advanced Components

# Chapter 14 — Rich Media & Content Components

---

# 14.1 Introduction

Modern enterprise platforms are no longer text-only systems.

Marketing teams publish campaigns.

Support agents exchange images.

Sales teams share presentations.

AI generates videos.

Designers manage brand assets.

React Commerce enables businesses to create, manage, preview, and distribute rich media across multiple communication channels.

A consistent media system ensures every asset remains organized, accessible, and ready for reuse.

---

# 14.2 Design Philosophy

Rich media components follow six principles.

## Visual First

Media should be understood before it is opened.

Every asset requires a meaningful preview.

---

## Context Matters

Show where media is used.

Example

```text
Product Image

Used In

✓ WhatsApp Template

✓ Facebook Ad

✓ Website Banner
```

---

## Reusability

Every uploaded asset should be reusable throughout the platform.

Avoid duplicate uploads.

---

## Performance

Large assets should load progressively.

Never block the interface.

---

## Accessibility

Alternative text should be encouraged wherever appropriate.

---

## Content Lifecycle

Media progresses through stages.

```text
Upload

↓

Review

↓

Publish

↓

Archive
```

---

# 14.3 Rich Media Components

React Commerce supports:

| Component | Purpose |
|------------|-----------------------------|
| Image Card | Image Preview |
| Video Card | Video Preview |
| Audio Player | Audio Playback |
| Document Preview | PDFs & Files |
| Media Gallery | Asset Collection |
| Attachment List | Uploaded Files |
| Carousel | Multiple Media |
| File Uploader | Asset Import |
| Asset Picker | Media Selection |

---

# 14.4 Media Card

Media cards represent individual assets.

Blueprint

```text
┌──────────────────────────────┐

🖼

Image Preview

──────────────────────────────

Summer Banner.jpg

4.2 MB

Used in 8 Campaigns

──────────────────────────────

Preview

Download

More

└──────────────────────────────┘
```

Each card communicates:

- Preview
- File Name
- Size
- Type
- Usage
- Available Actions

---

# 14.5 Image Preview

Images should load progressively.

States

```text
Loading

↓

Thumbnail

↓

High Resolution

↓

Fullscreen
```

Support:

✔ Zoom

✔ Pan

✔ Rotate

✔ Fullscreen

---

# 14.6 Video Player

The default video player should support:

✔ Play

✔ Pause

✔ Seek

✔ Volume

✔ Captions

✔ Playback Speed

✔ Fullscreen

Example

```text
▶

────────────────────────

00:15 / 01:42

🔊

⚙

⛶
```

Remember playback position when reopening.

---

# 14.7 Audio Player

Audio components support:

- Voice Notes
- AI Audio
- Podcasts
- Call Recordings

Blueprint

```text
▶

──────────────

00:12 / 03:25

1×

Download
```

Playback should continue while users navigate the application, when appropriate.

---

# 14.8 Document Preview

Supported formats include:

- PDF
- DOCX
- XLSX
- PPTX
- TXT
- CSV

Preview before download whenever possible.

Example

```text
Quarterly Report.pdf

18 Pages

Preview

Download
```

---

# 14.9 File Upload

Uploading is one of the highest-frequency interactions.

Supported methods

✔ Drag & Drop

✔ Browse

✔ Paste

✔ Mobile Upload

Blueprint

```text
────────────────────────────

Drop files here

or

Browse Files

────────────────────────────
```

---

# 14.10 Upload Progress

Every upload displays progress.

```text
Uploading

██████████░░░░░

72%

Cancel
```

Provide:

- Progress
- Remaining Size
- Retry
- Cancel

---

# 14.11 Upload Validation

Validate before uploading.

Check:

- File Type
- File Size
- Resolution
- Duration
- Duplicate Assets

Prevent invalid uploads early.

---

# 14.12 Attachment List

Attachments appear consistently.

Example

```text
📄 Invoice.pdf

2.4 MB

────────────────

🖼 Banner.png

840 KB

────────────────

🎥 Demo.mp4

14 MB
```

Every attachment includes:

- Icon
- Name
- Size
- Status

---

# 14.13 Media Gallery

Media galleries organize assets visually.

Blueprint

```text
□□□□□□□□□□□□□□□□

□□□□□□□□□□□□□□□□

□□□□□□□□□□□□□□□□
```

Support:

✔ Grid View

✔ List View

✔ Search

✔ Filters

✔ Sorting

---

# 14.14 Asset Picker

Users should select existing assets without uploading duplicates.

Example

```text
Search Assets

Banner

Logo

Product

Videos

Recently Used
```

Frequently used assets should appear first.

---

# 14.15 Carousel

Carousels present multiple related assets.

Example

```text
◀

Image

Image

Image

▶
```

Limit carousels to related media.

Avoid excessive scrolling.

---

# 14.16 AI Generated Content

AI-generated assets should be clearly identified.

Example

```text
✨ Generated by React AI

Yesterday
```

Available actions

- Edit
- Regenerate
- Approve
- Publish

Users should distinguish AI-generated content from manually uploaded assets.

---

# 14.17 Media Metadata

Every asset stores metadata.

Examples

- Filename
- Uploaded By
- Upload Date
- Dimensions
- Duration
- File Size
- MIME Type
- Usage Count

Metadata should be searchable.

---

# 14.18 Version History

Media assets often evolve.

Example

```text
Version 4

↓

Version 3

↓

Version 2

↓

Version 1
```

Allow comparison and restoration.

Never overwrite files permanently.

---

# 14.19 Content Status

Every asset belongs to one status.

```text
Draft

↓

Review

↓

Approved

↓

Published

↓

Archived
```

Status should be visible throughout the interface.

---

# 14.20 Rich Text Editor

The editor supports:

✔ Headings

✔ Bold

✔ Italic

✔ Lists

✔ Links

✔ Images

✔ Tables

✔ Code Blocks

✔ Emojis

✔ Mentions

✔ Variables

✔ AI Writing

Toolbar

```text
B

I

U

•

1.

🔗

🖼

🤖
```

---

# 14.21 Variable Insertion

Dynamic content should be easily inserted.

Example

```text
Hello {{First Name}}

Your order {{Order Number}} has shipped.
```

Variables should be searchable.

---

# 14.22 Emoji Picker

Emoji selection supports:

✔ Search

✔ Categories

✔ Recent

✔ Skin Tone

Frequently used emojis should appear first.

---

# 14.23 Link Preview

When users paste links:

Automatically generate

```text
Title

Description

Thumbnail

Domain
```

Allow disabling previews.

---

# 14.24 Accessibility

Rich media components support:

✔ Alternative Text

✔ Captions

✔ Keyboard Controls

✔ Screen Readers

✔ Focus Indicators

✔ High Contrast

Every important visual should have a text equivalent.

---

# 14.25 Performance

Recommendations

✔ Lazy Loading

✔ Progressive Images

✔ Adaptive Streaming

✔ Image Compression

✔ CDN Delivery

✔ Thumbnail Generation

Media should never slow down the application.

---

# 14.26 Motion

Recommended animations

Image Hover

```text
120ms
```

Gallery Transition

```text
180ms
```

Video Open

```text
200ms
```

Upload Progress

```text
Real-Time
```

Motion should communicate state changes.

---

# 14.27 React Component API

```tsx

asset={image}

onPreview={handlePreview}

onDownload={handleDownload}

/>
```

Uploader

```tsx

multiple

dragDrop

/>
```

Rich Text Editor

```tsx
<RichTextEditor

variables

aiAssistant

attachments

/>
```

---

# 14.28 CSS Variables

```css
--media-card-radius:16px;

--media-card-padding:20px;

--gallery-gap:16px;

--upload-zone-height:220px;

--preview-backdrop:rgba(0,0,0,.65);
```

---

# 14.29 Do's & Don'ts

## Do

✔ Display thumbnails before downloads.

✔ Show upload progress.

✔ Preserve media metadata.

✔ Support version history.

✔ Encourage media reuse.

✔ Optimize performance.

✔ Clearly label AI-generated assets.

---

## Don't

✘ Force unnecessary downloads for previews.

✘ Hide upload failures.

✘ Overwrite assets silently.

✘ Allow duplicate uploads without warning.

✘ Display oversized media without optimization.

✘ Mix published and draft assets without distinction.

---

# 14.30 Chapter Summary

Rich Media & Content Components provide the foundation for creating, managing, and distributing digital assets throughout React Commerce.

By standardizing media cards, upload workflows, galleries, previews, document viewers, and AI-generated content, the platform ensures that every asset remains organized, discoverable, reusable, and accessible.

A unified media system enables teams to collaborate efficiently while maintaining brand consistency and operational performance.

---

# React Commerce Design Standard

> **Every media asset should be easy to discover, preview, reuse, and manage throughout its lifecycle. Rich media components must prioritize performance, accessibility, and clarity while supporting seamless collaboration across all React Commerce workflows.**

---

# End of Chapter 14 — Rich Media & Content Components
